\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ \left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket\in O\wedge{\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}

because
{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket}
.

·

Case
u<\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket
.

Since
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket
(because
p_{n+1}
is a pop, while
p_{i}
is a push), from

P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-concurrent-lifo}}

with
{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket}
, we get
{\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{o^{\prime}}
and
o^{\prime}<\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket
for some pop
o^{\prime}
.
But again,
o^{\prime}=\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
for some
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
, since the path matches
\leq
, which contradicts the second hypothesis.

Hence, we can assume the case
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket<u
.

Since
{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket}
, we have
u=\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
for some
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n

since the path matches
\leq
.

Now, we prove the conditions to use the IH on
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
. Let us consider the subpath
[p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}]
.

·

Suppose for a contradiction that
{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}
for some
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
.
But
{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket}
also, which implies
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket=\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket
by
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-pop-uniqueness}}
(Contradiction).

·

Let
\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U
with
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket
for
l\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
.

Since
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket<u=\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket
, by the second hypothesis on the
n+1
path instantiated on
\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket
,
we have
{\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}
for some
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1
.

If
k=n+1
, since
{\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket}
also, by
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-push-uniqueness}}

we have
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket=\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket
(Contradiction).

So,
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
.

By IH on the subpath,

S_{n}={\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

where we can strengthen
\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}
to
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1
,
because
\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket
is already observing
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
, i.e.,

S_{n}={\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

But
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
and by the third hypothesis on the
n+1
path, every push after
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket
is observed, meaning
that every push between
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket
and
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
is observed, hence,

\displaystyle\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]=

\displaystyle\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]\mathbin{{+}\mspace{-8mu}{+}}[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\ ]

and so,

S_{n}={\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::{\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

when we reverse the lists.

But since
p_{n+1}
is a pop,

S_{n+1}={\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

–

Case
p_{n+1}=push(v^{\prime})
for some
v^{\prime}
.

Suppose for a contradiction that
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
.
Since
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket
, by the third hypothesis on the
n+1
path,
{\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}
for some
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1
(Contradiction).
Therefore,
i=n+1
.

In order to use IH on the subpath
[p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}]
, we do a case analysis on
p_{n}
.

*

Case
p_{n}=pop()
.

By
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-return-completion}}
,
the post-condition predicate holds for
\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket
at some
v
.

·

Case
v\neq\textsc{EMPTY}
.

Hence,
{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket}
and
v={u}.\textit{in}
for some push
u
. Which means
u=\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
for
j<_{\mathbb{N}}n
(which means
1<_{\mathbb{N}}n
).

We now prove the conditions of IH restricted to the path
[p_{1},\ldots,p_{n-1}]
.

We cannot have
{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}
for some
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n-1
because
\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket
already
observes
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
.

Now, let push
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
such that
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
for
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n-1
.
Since
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket
, by
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-concurrent-lifo}}
,

{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{o^{\prime}}
and
o^{\prime}<\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket
for some
o^{\prime}
,
which means
o^{\prime}=\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket
for some
l\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n-1
.

Therefore, we can use IH on the
n-1
subpath,

S_{n}={\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n-1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

But
p_{n}
is a pop and
p_{n+1}=p_{i}
is a push, so

S_{n+1}={\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n-1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

But we already proved that any push
p_{k}
(for
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n-1
) after
p_{j}
is observed, and since
p_{j}
is
observed by
p_{n}
, we have,

\displaystyle\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n-1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]=

\displaystyle\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]

So,

S_{n+1}={\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

·

Case
v=\textsc{EMPTY}
.

By
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-return-completion}}
,

\forall u.\ u<\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket\implies\exists o.\ {u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{o}\wedge o<\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket

In particular, for any push
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
(for
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
), there is pop
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
(with
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
)
such that
{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket
.

So by part 2 of the IH on the subpath
[p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}]
,
S_{n}=[\ ]
. But since
p_{n+1}=p_{i}
is a push,
we have,

S_{n+1}={\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::[\ ]

But every push before
p_{n}
is observed (equivalently, every push before
p_{n+1}=p_{i}
is observed, as
p_{n}
is a pop),
hence,

\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]=[\ ]

and so,

S_{n+1}={\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

*

Case
p_{n}=push(v^{\prime\prime})
for some
v^{\prime\prime}
.

We have trivially that
p_{n}
is not observed in the path
[p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}]
, and every push
p_{k}
(for
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
)
after
p_{n}
is observed (satisfied vacuously).

Therefore, by the IH,

S_{n}={\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

But
p_{n+1}=p_{i}
is a push, so,

S_{n+1}={\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::{\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

But
p_{n}
is not observed in the
n+1
path, so

\displaystyle{\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{n}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}=

\displaystyle\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

which means,

S_{n+1}={\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]^{-1}

Proof of Part 2.

Suppose that for any push
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket
(for
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1
),
there is a pop
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
(
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1
) such that
{\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}
.
We need to show
S_{n+1}=[\ ]
.

If
p_{n+1}
is a push, by the hypothesis there is a pop
\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1
that observes it, which is impossible.
Therefore,
p_{n+1}
is a pop.

By
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-return-completion}}
,
the post-condition predicate holds for
\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket
at some
v
.

–

Case
v\neq\textsc{EMPTY}
. We have
{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket}
and
v={u}.\textit{in}
for some push
u
.
Hence,
u=\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
for some
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
.

We now prove the conditions for Part 1 on the IH for the subpath
[p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}]
.

Suppose for a contradiction that
{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}
(
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
). But

{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket}
also, which means
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket=\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket
(Contradiction).

Now, let push
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
such that
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
(
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
).
Since
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket
, by
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-concurrent-lifo}}
,

{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{o^{\prime}}
and
o^{\prime}<\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket

for some
o^{\prime}
, which means that
p_{k}
is observed in the
n
subpath.

Therefore, by the first part of the IH, we have
S_{n}={\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::L^{-1}
, where

L=\left[\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}\mid\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket\in U\wedge\neg\exists k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n.\ {\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\ \right]

We claim that
L=[\ ]
. For suppose for a contradiction that
L
contains some push
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket

such that
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
and
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
is not observed in the
n
subpath.

But by hypothesis of part 2,
{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{l}}\right\rrbracket}
(for
l\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n+1
) for some
p_{l}
.
But
p_{k}
is not observed in the
n
subpath, therefore
l=n+1
. But
{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket}
also,
so
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket=\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
(Contradiction).

Therefore
L=[\ ]
, and since
p_{n+1}
is a pop, we have,

S_{n+1}=[\ ]

–

Case
v=\textsc{EMPTY}
. By
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-return-completion}}
,

\forall u.\ u<\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket\implies\exists o.\ {u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{o}\wedge o<\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket

We now prove the hypothesis of the second part in IH for the subpath
[p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}]
.
Let push
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
(
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
). By the the above,
{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{o}
and
o<\left\llbracket{p_{n+1}}\right\rrbracket

for some pop
o
. Hence
o=\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
(
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
).

Hence, by the second part of IH on the
n
subpath,
S_{n}=[\ ]
. But
p_{n+1}
is an empty pop,
and so
S_{n+1}=[\ ]
.

Lemma 22

(Path Existence)
.

Let
\leq
be the total order of Lemma
20
.
For any
1\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n\leq_{\mathbb{N}}\lvert\overline{T}\rvert
,
there is a path in
Op^{S}
of length
n
that matches
\leq
and starts from the empty stack.

Proof A.10
.

By induction on
n
.

•

Case
n=1
. Denote by
x_{1}
the first event in
\leq
.

If
x_{1}
has terminated, then it cannot be a non-empty pop,
because
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-return-completion}}

and the definition of the postcondition predicate force
x_{1}

to observe another event. This observed event
must be in
\overline{T}
because
\overline{T}
is
\prec
-downward closed
(Lemma
14
).
Therefore, there must exist an event occurring
before

x_{1}
in
\leq
, which is impossible.

Therefore,
x_{1}
must be either a push or an unterminated pop, or
a terminated empty pop.

If
x_{1}
is a push,

[\ ]\xrightarrow{push(v)\ \left\langle tt\right\rangle}v::[\ ]
is a path
of length
1
starting from the empty stack that matches
\leq
.

If
x_{1}
is an unterminated pop or a terminated empty pop,

[\ ]\xrightarrow{push(v)\ \left\langle\textsc{EMPTY}\right\rangle}[\ ]
is a path
of length
1
starting from the empty stack that matches
\leq
.

•

Inductive case. Let
n\geq_{\mathbb{N}}1
. Suppose

n+1\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\overline{T}|
.
We need to show that there is a matching path of length
n+1
.
Since
1\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\overline{T}|
,
the inductive hypothesis implies that there is a matching path of length
n
,

\displaystyle S_{0}=[\ ]\xrightarrow{p_{1}(in_{1})\ \langle out_{1}\rangle}S_{1}\xrightarrow{p_{2}(in_{2})\ \langle out_{2}\rangle}\ldots\xrightarrow{p_{n}(in_{n})\ \langle out_{n}\rangle}S_{n}

(10)

We need to show that we can extend this path with a matching
n+1
step
for the
n+1
event in
\leq
. Denote the
n+1
event in
\leq
as
x_{n+1}
.

We do a case analysis on
x_{n+1}
,

–

Case
x_{n+1}=pop()
. By
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-return-completion}}

the postcondition holds for some
v
.

*

Case
v=\textsc{EMPTY}
. So,

\forall u.\ u<x_{n+1}\implies\exists o.\ {u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{o}\wedge o<x_{n+1}

From this and the fact that the path
[p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}]
matches
\leq
, we have
that for any push
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket
(
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
) there is a pop
\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket

such that
{\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket}
.

Therefore, by part 2 of Lemma
21
,

S_{n}=[\ ]
, and we can augment the path with

S_{n}\xrightarrow{pop()\ \left\langle\textsc{EMPTY}\right\rangle}[\ ]
.

In case
x_{n+1}\in T
, we know from

P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-return-completion}}
that

\textsc{EMPTY}=v={x_{n+1}}.\textit{out}
,
and the step matches
\leq
.

*

Case
v\neq\textsc{EMPTY}
. So,

\exists u.\ {u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{x_{n+1}}\wedge v={u}.\textit{in}

From this and the fact that the path
[p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}]
matches
\leq
, we have

u=\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket
, for some
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
.

We know show that the conditions for Part 1 in Lemma
21
hold.

·

Suppose for a contradiction that there is a pop
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
(
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
)
such that
{\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}
.

Since
{\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{x_{n+1}}
also, from
I_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-pop-uniqueness}}
, it follows

\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket=x_{n+1}
(Contradiction).

·

Suppose a push
\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
(
k\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
) with
\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket<\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket
.

By
P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-concurrent-lifo}}
with
{\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{x_{n+1}}
,
there is pop
o^{\prime}
such that
{\left\llbracket{p_{k}}\right\rrbracket}\mathrel{\lessdot}{o^{\prime}}
and
o^{\prime}<x_{n+1}
.
Therefore,
o^{\prime}=\left\llbracket{p_{j}}\right\rrbracket
, for
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}n
.

There by Part 1 in Lemma
21
,

S_{n}={\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}::L

for some
L
.

Hence, we make the step
S_{n}\xrightarrow{pop()\ \left\langle v\right\rangle}L
,
since
v={u}.\textit{in}={\left\llbracket{p_{i}}\right\rrbracket}.\textit{in}
.

In case
x_{n+1}\in T
, we know from

P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-return-completion}}
that

v={x_{n+1}}.\textit{out}
,
and the step matches
\leq
.

–

Case
x_{n+1}=push(v)
.

We make the step
S_{n}\xrightarrow{push(v)\ \left\langle tt\right\rangle}v::S_{n}
.
In case
x_{n+1}\in T
, we know from

P_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::lin::cc-return-completion}}
that

{x_{n+1}}.\textit{out}=tt
,
and the step matches
\leq
.

Lemma 23

(Sequential Soundness)
.

The total order
\leq
of Lemma
20
is sequentially sound.

Proof A.11
.

If
\overline{T}=\emptyset
, then the empty path matches
\leq
.
If
\overline{T}\neq\emptyset
, then Lemma
22

applied with
n=|\overline{T}|
ensures the existence of a matching path
for
\leq
.

Theorem 24

(Stack Linearizability)
.

Let
D
be an arbitrary implementation of a concurrent stack.
Suppose that for any set of
abstract events
Ev
generated from an arbitrary execution
history in
D
, there are relations

\lessdot
,

\ltimes
definable using
D
such that
the visibility-style axioms in Figure
3
hold. Then,
D
is linearizable.

Proof A.12
.

Let
Ev
be a set of events generated from an arbitrary
execution history in the implementation.
From the hypothesis, the visibility-style axioms hold
for the relations
\lessdot
,

\ltimes
.
Let
\leq
be the total order of Lemma

20
(which depends
on the visibility-style axioms). Then, we take the
constraint relation
\prec
(i.e.,
\prec\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\lessdot\cup\ltimes
)
and
\leq
to be the
relations required by the definition of linearizability.
By construction,
\leq
respects both
\prec
and

\sqsubset
. Also,

\leq
is sequentially sound by Lemma

23
.

Appendix B
Proof of the Visibility-style Axioms

In this appendix we show that both versions of the TS-stack
(atomic timestamps and interval timestamps)
satisfy the visibility-style axioms
of Figure
3
.

The proof is divided in two parts. First, we show that
the visibility-style axioms follow from the TS-stack invariants in
Figure
10
(Section
B.1
),
as this part of the proof is common to both versions of the TS-stack.
Then we show that both versions of the TS-stack satisfy the
TS-stack invariants (Section
B.2
).

B.1
Proof of Axioms from TS-stack invariants

This step is divided into two. Section
B.1.2

proves that the visibility-style axioms
hold when elimination pairs are removed from
\overline{T}
.
Section
B.1.3

then shows that the visibility-style axioms still hold when the elimination
pairs are put back into
\overline{T}
.
Section
B.1.1
provides
common definitions, the TS-stack algorithm and presents the TS-stack invariants.

We now define some concepts that are common to both versions of the TS-stack.

Definition 25
.

We have the following definitions, which refer to the code in
Figures
6
and

7
.

Events and Rep Events

For event
e
, we denote by
{e}.\textit{start}
,
{e}.\textit{end}
,

{e}.\textit{in}
,
{e}.\textit{out}
the start time, end time, input, and output of event
e
,
respectively.

{e}.\textit{end}
and
{e}.\textit{out}
remain undefined if
e
has not terminated.

Similarly, for rep event
r
, we denote by
{r}.\textit{time}
,
{r}.\textit{in}
,
{r}.\textit{out}

the time, input, and output of rep event
r
, respectively. The three properties
are always defined for rep events, because rep events are atomic.
Additionally, we denote by
<
the strict total order on rep events.

Spans

Spans are pairs of rep events
(a,b)
. For span
s
, we denote by
start\,({s})

and
end\,({s})
the first and second projection functions.

We define the order on spans as,

s_{1}\sqsubset^{S}s_{2}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}end\,({s_{1}})<start\,({s_{2}})

and denote by
\sqsubseteq^{S}
its reflexive closure.

Span Function (
\mathcal{S}
)

Function
\mathcal{S}\ {e}
returns the unique
span executed by event
e
. More specifically,

For a push event
u
,
\mathcal{S}\ {u}
returns the span starting when
the new node is
linked as first node of the pool
(line
63
). The span
ends when a finite timestamp is assigned to the new node
(line
7
).

For a pop event
o
,
\mathcal{S}\ {o}
returns the span starting before
the pools loop (line
16
) as long as this line
executed before the
last
pools loop. The span ends on the successful
CAS at line
71
which takes a
node for the pop to return.

If event
e
has not completed its span,
\mathcal{S}\ {e}
is undefined.

Node Id Function (
id
)

Function
\textit{id}\ {e}
returns the unique
node id generated by
e
(if
e
is a push) or the unique node id of the taken node
(if
e
is a pop). More specifically,

For a push event
u
,
\textit{id}\ {u}
returns the id of the generated node at
line
62
as soon
as the node is linked to the pool at line
63
.
For pop event
o
,
\textit{id}\ {o}

returns the id of the taken node at line
71
.

If event
e
has not executed the mentioned lines,
\textit{id}\ {e}
is undefined.

Timestamp Function (
t_{s}
)

Function
t_{s}\ {e}
returns the
timestamp generated by
e
(if
e
is a push) or the timestamp of the taken node
(if
e
is a pop). More specifically,

For a push
u
,
t_{s}\ {u}
returns the generated timestamp as soon as it is assigned to the node at line
7
.

For a pop
o
,
t_{s}\ {o}

returns the timestamp (as was read at line
24
)
of the taken node at line
71
.

If event
e
has not executed the mentioned lines,
t_{s}\ {e}
is undefined.
Note that
t_{s}\ {e}
is defined if and only if
\mathcal{S}\ {e}
is defined,
because
e
completes its span precisely when the mentioned lines in the
definition of
t_{s}\ {e}
execute.

Timestamp Order (
<_{\textsc{T}}
)

The relation
<_{\textsc{T}}

denotes the strict partial order on timestamps.

For the atomic TS-stack,
<_{\textsc{T}}

is simply the strict total order on natural numbers
<_{\mathbb{N}}
,
extended with minimum value
-\infty
and maximum value
\infty
.

For the interval TS-stack,

<_{\textsc{T}}
is the strict partial order on interval timestamps defined as,

[a,b]<_{\textsc{T}}[c,d]\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}b<_{\mathbb{N}}c

and extended with a minimum value
-\infty
and maximum value
\infty
.

Abstract Timestamp Function (
at_{s}
)

For event
e
,

at_{s}\ {e}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}(\textit{id}\ {e},t_{s}\ {e})
returns a pair
combining the id and timestamp of
e
. If either
\textit{id}\ {e}
or
t_{s}\ {e}
is undefined,
then
at_{s}\ {e}
is undefined.

We call the pairs
(i,t)
, which combine a natural number
i

(representing an id) and a timestamp
t
,
abstract timestamps
,
and we overload the timestamp order
<_{\textsc{T}}
into abstract timestamps
in the natural way, as there is no risk of confusion,

(i_{1},t_{1})<_{\textsc{T}}(i_{2},t_{2})\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}t_{1}<_{\textsc{T}}t_{2}

Output Function (
O
)

Function
\textsc{O}({e})
returns the
output associated with the possibly not terminated event
e
. More specifically,

For a push event
u
,
\textsc{O}({u})
returns
tt

if
u
has generated an id and linked the new node at
line
63
.

For a pop event
o
,
\textsc{O}({o})
returns the value stored in the taken node
at line
71
.

If event
e
has not executed the mentioned lines,
\textsc{O}({e})
is undefined.

Do not confuse
\textsc{O}({e})
with
{e}.\textit{out}
. Function
{e}.\textit{out}

is defined only when
e
has terminated, while
\textsc{O}({e})
describes
the output that is going to be chosen when
e
is included in the completion
\overline{T}
,
even if
e
has not terminated. For terminated events
e
, we will have

{e}.\textit{out}=\textsc{O}({e})
.

Elimination pairs

We define when push
u
and pop
o
form an elimination pair.

{u}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {o}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {o}\wedge u\not\sqsubset o

In English: (1)
o
pops the node that
u
pushed
(
\textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {o}
), and (2)
u
and
o
overlap. Events
u

and
o
overlap if
u\not\sqsubset o
and

o\not\sqsubset u
, but it is not necessary to explicitly
check
o\not\sqsubset u
, as that follows from

\textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {o}
and a structural invariant that
o
cannot
pop a node that has not been pushed yet. We shall present the invariants soon.

Elimination Pair Events Set (
E
)

We define the set
of events occurring in elimination pairs as,

E\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{x\mid\exists y.\ {x}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {y}\vee{y}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {x}\}

Pop-Totality

We assume that we are given a strict partial order on
abstract timestamps
\ll
, such that it is
pop-total
,

\displaystyle\forall u_{1}\ u_{2}\ o\notin E.

\displaystyle(at_{s}\ {u_{1}}=at_{s}\ {o})\vee(at_{s}\ {u_{2}}=at_{s}\ {o})\implies

\displaystyle(at_{s}\ {u_{1}})\ \ll\ (at_{s}\ {u_{2}})\vee(at_{s}\ {u_{2}})\ \ll\ (at_{s}\ {u_{1}})\vee(at_{s}\ {u_{1}}=at_{s}\ {u_{2}})

As usual, we denote by
\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}
its reflexive closure.

Pop-totality is a weaker form of totality for a partial order, in which two
abstract timestamps are required to be totally comparable only when one of the pushes
that generated them is taken by a pop.

The property of pop-totality will be used to prove theorems where elimination pairs
are excluded. Hence, we explicitly exclude elimination pairs with the condition
\notin E

in the universally quantified variables of the property.

Sections
B.2.1

and
B.2.2

will define
\ll
for the atomic and interval
cases, respectively.

In what follows, we assume that variables
o
and
u
, and their
variations, range over pop and push events, respectively.
Variables
x
,
y
range over arbitrary events.

We have these immediate lemmas from the definitions,

Lemma 26
.

<_{\textsc{T}}
overloaded to abstract timestamps is a strict partial order.

Proof B.13
.

We prove each property,

Irreflexivity. If
(i,t)<_{\textsc{T}}(i,t)
, then
t<_{\textsc{T}}t
(Contradiction).

Transitivity. If
(i_{1},t_{1})<_{\textsc{T}}(i_{2},t_{2})
and
(i_{2},t_{2})<_{\textsc{T}}(i_{3},t_{3})
, then

t_{1}<_{\textsc{T}}t_{2}
and
t_{2}<_{\textsc{T}}t_{3}
, which means
t_{1}<_{\textsc{T}}t_{3}
.
Therefore
(i_{1},t_{1})<_{\textsc{T}}(i_{3},t_{3})
by definition.

Lemma 27
.

The following holds,

1.

If
u
is a push such that
u\notin E
, then

\forall o.\ \textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {o}\implies u\sqsubset o
.

2.

If
o
is a pop such that
o\notin E
, then

\forall u.\ \textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {o}\implies u\sqsubset o
.

Proof B.14
.

We prove each part,

1.

Since
u\notin E
, then
\forall y.\ \neg({u}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {y})\wedge\neg({y}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {u})
.
In particular,
\forall y.\ \neg({u}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {y})
.

After some De Morgan manipulations on the definition of
Elim
,

\forall y.\ y\text{ is a pop}\wedge\textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {y}\implies u\sqsubset y

and so,
\forall o.\ \textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {o}\implies u\sqsubset o
directly.

2.

Similar to the previous case.

We now define the visibility and separability relations.

First, we define the base relations, which are those
in Section
4.2
,
but they have been abstracted to use

at_{s}
and
\ll
, instead of

t_{s}
and
<_{\textsc{T}}
; also, the exclusion
of elimination pairs have been made explicit with the clause
\notin E
.

Definition 28

(Base Relations)
.

\displaystyle{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}^{B}{o}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle u,o\notin E\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u}=at_{s}\ {o}

\displaystyle{u_{1}}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{u_{2}}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle u_{1},u_{2}\notin E\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u_{1}}\ll at_{s}\ {u_{2}}

\displaystyle{o}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{u}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle o,u\notin E\ \wedge\ \exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {o}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {u}

\displaystyle\textsc{Miss}\ {o}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle o,u^{\prime}\notin E\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {o}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\forall o^{\prime}\notin E.\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}=at_{s}\ {o^{\prime}}\implies{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o^{\prime}}})}

\displaystyle{o_{2}}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{o_{1}}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle o_{2},o_{1}\notin E\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {o_{1}}\ll at_{s}\ {o_{2}}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {o_{1}}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {o_{2}}

We next define the relations that extend the base relations to include
elimination pairs. We define them as a recursively defined indexed family

\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}
and

\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}
.
The intuition behind the
index
i
in
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}
and

\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}
is that the
i
-th elimination
pair has been added to the relations
(the elimination pairs are enumerated from 0 to
|\textsc{Elim}|-1
, where

|\textsc{Elim}|
is the number of pairs in the relation
Elim
).
The zero indexed relations are simply the base relations,
which exclude all elimination pairs.

Definition 29

(Elimination Pair Relations)
.

Given the definitions,

\displaystyle P_{i}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{\pi_{1}({\textsc{Elim}_{i}}),\pi_{2}({\textsc{Elim}_{i}})\}

\displaystyle E_{0}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}E

\displaystyle E_{i+1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}E_{i}\setminus P_{i}\qquad\text{ for }i<_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|

where
\textsc{Elim}_{i}
is an enumeration of the pairs in
Elim
, from 0 to
|\textsc{Elim}|-1
.
11
11

11

Any enumeration will work.

And
\pi_{1}({p})
,
\pi_{2}({p})
are the standard projection functions for pair
p
.
Also, given
{u}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {o}
, we define
C\ {u}=o
and
C\ {o}=u
, i.e.,
C\ {e}
is
the event that is coupled with
e
in the elimination pair.

We define the visibility and separability relations recursively, for
i<_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|
,

\displaystyle{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{0}{o}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}^{B}{o}

\displaystyle{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{0}{y}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}

\displaystyle{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i+1}{o}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}{o}\vee\textsc{Elim}_{i}=(u,o)

\displaystyle{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i+1}{y}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle(x\notin E_{i}\wedge y\in P_{i}\wedge\textsc{BE}_{i}\ {x}\ {y})\vee(x\in P_{i}\wedge y\notin E_{i}\wedge\neg\textsc{BE}_{i}\ {y}\ {x})\vee{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}{y}

\displaystyle\textsc{BE}_{i}\ {x}\ {y}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle y\in E\wedge\exists z.\ x\mathrel{{\prec_{i}}^{*}}z\wedge({z}\sqsubset{C\ {y}}\vee{z}\sqsubset{y})

\displaystyle\prec_{i}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}\cup\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}

where
\mathrel{{\prec_{i}}^{*}}
is the reflexive and transitive closure of
\prec_{i}
.
The closure
\mathrel{{\prec_{i}}^{*}}
is computed over
Ev
.

The indexed set
P_{i}
collects the events in the
i
-th elimination pair
(remember that the pairs in relation
Elim
are enumerated by
\textsc{Elim}_{i}
).

The family of sets
E_{i}
will be useful to state situations when
we want to express “all elimination pairs but the first
i
ones”.
Set
E_{0}
contains all events belonging to an elimination pair, since it is
simply
E
.
Set
E_{i+1}
is simply
E_{i}
with the
i
-th elimination pair removed.
So, for example,
E_{0}
has all the elimination pairs;
E_{1}
removes from
E_{0}
the
0-th elimination pair (remember that the pairs in relation
Elim
are enumerated
by
\textsc{Elim}_{i}
starting from index 0);
E_{2}
removes from
E_{1}
the
1-th elimination pair, or equivalently,
E_{2}
removes from
E_{0}
the
0-th and 1-th elimination pairs; and so on.

The family of relations
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}
and
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}
are the visibility and
separability relations when the first
i
elimination pairs have been included.
Relations
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{0}
and
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{0}
encode
the visibility and separability relations when
no
elimination
pairs are included.

Relation
{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i+1}{o}
states that

u
is observed by
o
if either they were already related
in this way in the previous stage
{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}{o}
,
or
(u,o)
is the
i
-th elimination pair in the enumeration,
since the push in an elimination pair is taken by the pop
in the elimination pair,
and hence, the push is observed by the pop.

To understand
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i+1}{y}
, it is necessary
to explain how separation works in the presence of elimination
pairs.
The key property of an elimination pair is that it can be freely
ordered anywhere in a linearization, because it will not affect
the abstract state of the stack. The reason for this is that an elimination
pair is linearized as a push immediately followed by a pop. So, if
S
is the stack
before executing the elimination pair, after executing the push and
pop in the elimination pair, the stack is again
S
.
Since the push and pop in an elimination pair are linearized as
one immediately followed by the other, the push and pop
in an elimination pair can be treated as a single unit in any argument.

However, linearizability must respect the returns-before relation.
Therefore, elimination pairs can be freely ordered as long as returns-before
is respected.
This means that an event
x
that returns-before

u

or

o
in the elimination pair
(u,o)
,
should be ordered before
both
events
u
and
o
,
since we treat the push and pop in the elimination pair as a single unit.

Figure 9
:
Possible execution showing four events. Push
u
and pop
o

form an elimination pair. Event
z
returns-before
o
, and
x

overlaps with
z
,
u
, and
o
.
Event
x
is a dependency of
z
under the constraint relation

\prec_{0}
at level 0,
indicated with a dashed arrow.

However, the above intuition is still incomplete
because it does not capture all the possible
situations in which
x
should be ordered before both
u
and
o
.
To complete the intuition, the key
insight is to also take into account dependencies under the constraint
relation
\prec
.
For example, Figure
9

shows an execution in which
x
is a dependency at level 0
of some event
z
(i.e.,
x\prec_{0}z
, where
\prec_{0}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{0}\cup\mathrel{\ltimes}_{0}
)
and
z
returns-before
o
. Even though
x

does not
return-before

u
or
o
, event
x
should still be ordered before
u
and
o
because
(1)
x
is a dependency of
z
, hence,
x
should be ordered before
z
and
(2)
z
returns-before the elimination pair, hence,
z

should be ordered before
u
and
o
.

We can formally capture the above idea by saying that

x

should be ordered before
elimination pair event
y
at level 0,
denoted
\textsc{BE}_{0}\ {x}\ {y}
(we will later generalize to an arbitrary level
i
) if,

\displaystyle\textsc{BE}_{0}\ {x}\ {y}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}y\in E\wedge\exists z.\ x\mathrel{{\prec_{0}}^{*}}z\wedge({z}\sqsubset{C\ {y}}\vee{z}\sqsubset{y})

In English:
y
is an elimination pair event and

x
is a dependency at level 0 of some event
z
that either returns-before
the elimination couple of
y
or returns-before
y
.
We use the reflexive-transitive closure
\mathrel{{\prec_{0}}^{*}}
to account
for the fact that
z
could be
x
. Hence, in
Figure
9

we have both
\textsc{BE}_{0}\ {x}\ {u}
and
\textsc{BE}_{0}\ {x}\ {o}
.

With the
BE
predicate defined at level 0, we can now think how we should define the separability
relation at level 1 (i.e.,
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{1}
).
At level 1 we take into consideration the two events in the first elimination
pair in the enumeration of
Elim
, i.e., the two events in set

P_{0}
,
12
12

12

Remember that indexes in the enumeration
of
Elim
start from 0.

together with all the events that were previously related under
the visibility and separability relations at level 0, i.e. all non-elimination
pair events, or equivalently, all events
in the set
\textsc{Ev}\setminus E_{0}
.
Therefore, we can either separate
(1) an
x\notin E_{0}
before a
y\in P_{0}
,
(2) an
x\in P_{0}
before a
y\notin E_{0}
,
(3) an
x\notin E_{0}
before a
y\notin E_{0}
.
Note that there is no need to separate an
x\in P_{0}

before a
y\in P_{0}
because
x
and
y
would
belong to the same elimination pair and we treat elimination
pairs as a single unit.

For the (1) case, we simply define
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{1}{y}
if

\textsc{BE}_{0}\ {x}\ {y}
, i.e., if
x
should be ordered before the elimination pair event
y
.

For (2) we cannot use
\textsc{BE}_{0}\ {x}\ {y}
because the
BE
predicate requires
y

to be an elimination pair event. Also, even though
x
is an elimination pair event,
we cannot define
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{1}{y}
simply as
\textsc{BE}_{0}\ {y}\ {x}
because
if
y
actually returns-before
x
, the separability relation
will introduce a violation of axiom
C_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::cc-no-future-dependence}}
, i.e.,

y\sqsubset x\mathrel{\ltimes}_{1}y
.
The key insight is to define
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{1}{y}
if
\neg\textsc{BE}_{0}\ {y}\ {x}
, because the negation is actually
stating the following,

x\in E\implies\forall z.\ y\mathrel{{\prec_{0}}^{*}}z\implies({z}\not\sqsubset{C\ {x}}\wedge{z}\not\sqsubset{x})

In particular, since
x\in P_{0}\subseteq E
and by instantiating
z
with
y
, we obtain

{y}\not\sqsubset{C\ {x}}\wedge{y}\not\sqsubset{x}
, and more specifically

{y}\not\sqsubset{x}
. In other words, either
y
overlaps with
x
or

y
actually starts after
x
finished. In both cases, it makes sense to separate
x

before
y
.
13
13

13

In the case when
y
overlaps with
x
, it is ok to arbitrarily
separate
x
before
y
because elimination pairs can be freely ordered
as long as returns-before is respected. In this case, since
x
and
y
overlap, there is
no restriction between
x
and
y
regarding returns-before.

For (3) we simply separate
x
before
y
if they were already separated
at level 0, i.e.,
x\mathrel{\ltimes}_{0}y
.

Each one of the above definitions for the cases (1), (2) and (3) corresponds to each
of the three disjuncts in
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i+1}

(Definition
29
),
but specialized to
i=0
.

Now that we have the definition of
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{1}
, we can then define

\textsc{BE}_{1}
, identically as in level 0 but using the constraint relation at level 1,

\prec_{1}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{1}\cup\mathrel{\lessdot}_{1}
.
Then, we define
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{2}
identically as in level 1, but using
\textsc{BE}_{1}
and
sets
P_{1}
and
\textsc{Ev}\setminus E_{1}
. We use
P_{1}
because it contains the two events
in the second elimination pair in the enumeration, while set
\textsc{Ev}\setminus E_{1}

contains all non-elimination pairs together with the first elimination pair, which has been
already related by the visibility and separability relations at the previous level 1.
We can continue this construction process for arbitrary higher levels, so that
the general step is described by
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i+1}

as in Definition
29
,
for arbitrary
i
.

This process finishes when
i+1
reaches
|\textsc{Elim}|
, i.e.,
the total number of elimination pairs. Hence,
we define the final visibility and separability relations
precisely at this index,
which include all the elimination pairs,

Definition 30

(Final Visibility and Separability Relations)
.

\displaystyle\lessdot

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{|\textsc{Elim}|}

\displaystyle\ltimes

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{|\textsc{Elim}|}

Using
\lessdot
and
\ltimes
as in Definition
30
,
the proof that the TS-stack satisfies the visibility-style axioms
works as follows.
We prove inductively that for any natural number
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|
,
the visibility-style axioms hold when all the variables are restricted to the domain

\overline{T}\setminus E_{i}
. This means that when
i=|\textsc{Elim}|
,
all the visibility-style axioms hold at domain
\overline{T}\setminus E_{|\textsc{Elim}|}=\overline{T}
,
since we will show that
E_{|\textsc{Elim}|}=\emptyset
.

Section
B.1.2
proves the base case (
i=0
),
i.e., when all the elimination pairs are elided.
Section
B.1.3
then shows the inductive step:
if the axioms hold at domain
\overline{T}\setminus E_{i}

then they also hold at domain

\overline{T}\setminus E_{i+1}
.

(
I_{31}
) Disjoint Push Timestamp Generation

{\mathcal{S}\ {u_{1}}}\sqsubset^{S}{\mathcal{S}\ {u_{2}}}\implies at_{s}\ {u_{1}}\ll at_{s}\ {u_{2}}

(
I_{32}
) Misses start late

\textsc{Miss}\ {o}\ {u}\implies{start\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})}<{start\,({\mathcal{S}\ {u}})}

(a)

Key TS-stack invariants.

(
I_{33}
)

\textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {o}\implies{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})}.\textit{time}\not<_{\mathbb{N}}{u}.\textit{start}

(
I_{34}
)

\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})\implies{start\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}\leq{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}

(

\textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {o}\wedge{\mathcal{S}\ {u}}\sqsubset^{S}{\mathcal{S}\ {o}}\implies t_{s}\ {u}=t_{s}\ {o}

(
I_{36}
)

\textit{defined}\,({\textit{id}\ {o}})\implies\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})

(
I_{37}
)

\textit{defined}\,({t_{s}\ {x}})\implies\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})

(

x\in T\implies\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})\wedge{x}.\textit{out}=\textsc{O}({x})

(

\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})\implies\textit{defined}\,({t_{s}\ {x}})\wedge\textit{defined}\,({\textit{id}\ {x}})\wedge\textit{defined}\,({\textsc{O}({x})})

(

)

\mathcal{S}\ {x}=\mathcal{S}\ {y}\implies x=y

)

)

(i)
\textit{id}\ {u_{1}}=\textit{id}\ {u_{2}}\implies u_{1}=u_{2}

(ii)
\textit{id}\ {o_{1}}=\textit{id}\ {o_{2}}\implies o_{1}=o_{2}

(

)

(i)
\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})\implies{x}.\textit{start}\ \leq_{\mathbb{N}}\ {start\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}.\textit{time}

(ii)
\textit{defined}\,({{x}.\textit{end}})\implies{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}.\textit{time}\ \leq_{\mathbb{N}}\ {x}.\textit{end}

(

)

(i)
\textit{defined}\,({\textit{id}\ {o}})\implies\exists u.\ \textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {o}\wedge\textsc{O}({o})={u}.\textit{in}

(ii)
\textit{defined}\,({\textit{id}\ {u}})\implies\textsc{O}({u})=tt

(b)

Structural TS-stack invariants.

Figure 10
:
TS-stack invariants.

During the entire proof, we assume that all the TS-stack invariants
in Figure
10
hold.
In other words, we will prove the visibility-style axioms under the assumption
of these invariants. Later, Section
B.2

will show that both versions of the TS-stack satisfy the invariants.

Before starting the main proof, we have the following lemmas which follow
directly from the definitions.

Lemma 45
.

Let
i,j\in|\textsc{Elim}|
. If
i\neq j
and
\textsc{Elim}_{i}=(u_{i},o_{i})
and
\textsc{Elim}_{j}=(u_{j},o_{j})
, then
u_{i}\neq u_{j}
and
o_{i}\neq o_{j}
.

Proof B.15
.

Suppose for a contradiction that
u_{i}=u_{j}\vee o_{i}=o_{j}
. We focus on the
u_{i}=u_{j}
case, as the other one is similar.

Since
\textsc{Elim}_{i}=(u_{i},o_{i})
and
\textsc{Elim}_{j}=(u_{j},o_{j})
, we have by definition of the
Elim
relation that

\textit{id}\ {u_{i}}=\textit{id}\ {o_{i}}
and
\textit{id}\ {u_{j}}=\textit{id}\ {o_{j}}
. But
u_{i}=u_{j}
, which implies
\textit{id}\ {o_{i}}=\textit{id}\ {o_{j}}
.
So,
o_{i}=o_{j}
by
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::prop-funcs-are-injective}}
.
But then
\textsc{Elim}_{i}=(u_{i},o_{i})=(u_{j},o_{j})=\textsc{Elim}_{j}
, meaning that they are the same pair in
the enumeration, and so
i=j
(Contradiction).

Lemma 46
.

Let
i,j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|
.

1.

P_{i}\subseteq E_{i}
.

2.

If
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}i
, then
E_{i}\subseteq E_{j}
.

3.

If
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}i
, then
{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{j}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}}
and
{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{j}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}}
.

Proof B.16
.

We prove each part.

1.

Let
x\in P_{i}
. Since
x
is in the
i
-th elimination pair in the enumeration of
Elim
,
we have
x\in E_{0}
. Suppose for a contradiction
x\in\bigcup_{j<_{\mathbb{N}}i}P_{j}
. Then,
x
is in both
the
i
-th elimination pair and the
j
-th elimination pair (
j<_{\mathbb{N}}i
). So,
x\neq x
by Lemma
45
(Contradiction). Hence,
x\notin\bigcup_{j<_{\mathbb{N}}i}P_{j}
.

The result follows from the following claim when
l=i
.

{claim*}

Let
l\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|
. If
x\in E_{0}
and
x\notin\bigcup_{j<_{\mathbb{N}}l}P_{j}
,
then
x\in E_{l}
.

The base case (
l=0
) is immediate. For the inductive step, suppose

x\in E_{0}
and
x\notin\bigcup_{j<_{\mathbb{N}}l+1}P_{j}
. Hence,

x\notin\bigcup_{j<_{\mathbb{N}}l}P_{j}
and
x\notin P_{l}
.

By IH,
x\in E_{l}
. But from
x\notin P_{l}
, we get
x\in E_{l+1}
by
definition.

2.

By induction on
i
.

For the base case (
i=0
),
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}0
implies
j=0
, and
E_{0}\subseteq E_{0}
follows trivially.

For the inductive case, suppose
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}i+1
. If
j=i+1
, we have trivially
E_{i+1}\subseteq E_{i+1}=E_{j}
.

Hence, we can assume
j<_{\mathbb{N}}i+1
. So,
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}i
and by IH,
E_{i}\subseteq E_{j}
.
But if
x\in E_{i+1}
, then
x\in E_{i}
by definition. Therefore,
E_{i+1}\subseteq E_{j}
.

3.

By induction on
i
.

For the base case (
i=0
),
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}0
implies
j=0
, and
{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{0}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{0}}
and
{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{0}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{0}}
follow trivially.

For the inductive case, suppose
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}i+1
. If
j=i+1
, we have trivially
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{j}={\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i+1}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i+1}}
and
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{j}={\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i+1}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i+1}}
.

Hence, we can assume
j<_{\mathbb{N}}i+1
. So,
j\leq_{\mathbb{N}}i
and by IH,
{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{j}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}}
and
{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{j}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}}
.

But if
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}{y}
holds, then
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i+1}{y}
by definition. Similarly for
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}{y}
. Therefore,

{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{j}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i+1}}
and
{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{j}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i+1}}
.

Lemma 47
.

Let
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|
. Suppose
x,y\in\textsc{Ev}\setminus E_{i}
.

•

If
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}{y}
, then
x\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}y
.

•

If
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}{y}
, then
x\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}y
.

•

If
{x}\prec{y}
, then
x\prec_{i}y
.

Proof B.17
.

Suppose
x,y\in\textsc{Ev}\setminus E_{i}
. If either
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}{y}
, or
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}{y}
, or
{x}\prec{y}
,
then
x\mathrel{\lessdot}_{|\textsc{Elim}|}y
or
x\mathrel{\ltimes}_{|\textsc{Elim}|}y
by definition.

The result follows from the following claim when
l=|\textsc{Elim}|
. We prove the claim by induction on
l
.

{claim*}

Let
l\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|
.

•

If
x\mathrel{\lessdot}_{l}y
, then
x\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}y
.

•

If
x\mathrel{\ltimes}_{l}y
, then
x\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}y
.

The base case (
l=0
) follows directly from part 3 of Lemma
46
.

For the inductive case, suppose either
x\mathrel{\lessdot}_{l+1}y
or
x\mathrel{\ltimes}_{l+1}y
.
If
l<_{\mathbb{N}}i
, then
l+1\leq_{\mathbb{N}}i
, and by part 3 of Lemma
46
,

x\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}y
and
x\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}y
hold, respectively.

Hence, we can assume
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}l
. We now check each case in the definition of
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{l+1}
and
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{l+1}
.

•

Case
x
is a push and
y
is a pop, and
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{l}{y}
. By IH,
x\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}y
holds.

•

Case
x
is a push and
y
is a pop, and
\textsc{Elim}_{l}=(x,y)
. Then,
x,y\in P_{l}
by definition.

By part 1 of Lemma
46
, we have
x,y\in E_{l}
. But by
part 2 of Lemma
46
, we have
x,y\in E_{i}
(since
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}l
),
which contradicts hypothesis
x,y\in\textsc{Ev}\setminus E_{i}
.

•

Case
x\notin E_{l}\wedge y\in P_{l}\wedge\textsc{BE}_{l}\ {x}\ {y}
. But
y\in P_{l}

and part 1 of Lemma
46
imply
y\in E_{l}
.
Hence, part 2 of Lemma
46
implies
y\in E_{i}
(since
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}l
)
which contradicts hypothesis
y\in\textsc{Ev}\setminus E_{i}
.

•

Case
x\in P_{l}\wedge y\notin E_{l}\wedge\neg\textsc{BE}_{l}\ {y}\ {x}
. Similar to the previous case, but using
x
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{l}{y}
. By IH,
x\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}y
holds.

This proves the claim.

Lemma 48
.

Let
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|
. Let
x
a push and
y
a pop.
If
\textit{id}\ {x}=\textit{id}\ {y}
, then
x\in E_{i}
if and only if
y\in E_{i}
.

Proof B.18
.

By induction on
i
.

•

Base case
i=0
. We focus on the forward direction, since the other one is similar.

Since
x\in E_{0}
, by definition of
E_{0}
, there is a pop
o
such that
{x}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {o}
.
But this implies by definition of
Elim
that
\textit{id}\ {x}=\textit{id}\ {o}
. And hence,

\textit{id}\ {o}=\textit{id}\ {y}
since
\textit{id}\ {x}=\textit{id}\ {y}
by hypothesis.
Therefore,
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::prop-funcs-are-injective}}

implies
o=y
, meaning that
{x}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {y}
, and so
y\in E_{0}
by
definition.

•

Inductive case. We focus on the forward direction, since the other one is similar.

Since
x\in E_{i+1}
, we have
x\in E_{i}
and
x\notin P_{i}
by definition.
By IH,
y\in E_{i}
. Suppose for a contradiction that
y\in P_{i}
. Then,

\textsc{Elim}_{i}=(u,y)
for some push
u
by definition of
P_{i}
. But this means
{u}\ \textsc{Elim}\ {y}
.

So,
\textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {y}
by definition of
Elim
. And hence,

\textit{id}\ {u}=\textit{id}\ {x}
since
\textit{id}\ {x}=\textit{id}\ {y}
by hypothesis.
Therefore,
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::prop-funcs-are-injective}}

implies
u=x
, meaning that
\textsc{Elim}_{i}=(x,y)
, and so
x\in P_{i}
(Contradiction).

Hence,
y\notin P_{i}
, and so,
y\in E_{i+1}
by definition.

Lemma 49
.

Let
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|
. If
x\prec_{i}y
, then
x,y\notin E_{i}
.

Proof B.19
.

By induction on
i
.

The base case
(i=0)
follows directly from the definitions of
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{0}
and
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{0}
.
Hence, we can focus on the inductive case. We consider each case in the definition of
x\prec_{i+1}y
,

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{i}{y}
. From the IH, we have
x,y\notin E_{i}
. So,
x,y\notin E_{i+1}
,
since
E_{i+1}\subseteq E_{i}
by Lemma
46
.

•

Case
\textsc{Elim}_{i}=(x,y)
. Hence,
x,y\in P_{i}
by definition. This means that

x,y\notin E_{i+1}
, for otherwise we would have
x,y\notin P_{i}
by definition of
E_{i+1}
.

•

Case
x\notin E_{i}\wedge y\in P_{i}\wedge\textsc{BE}_{i}\ {x}\ {y}
. Since
y\in P_{i}
, this means that

y\notin E_{i+1}
, for otherwise we would have
y\notin P_{i}
by definition of
E_{i+1}
.

Also, since
x\notin E_{i}
, then
x\notin E_{i+1}
,
since
E_{i+1}\subseteq E_{i}
by Lemma
46
.

•

Case
x\in P_{i}\wedge y\notin E_{i}\wedge\neg\textsc{BE}_{i}\ {y}\ {x}
. Similar to the previous case, but
inverting the roles of
x
and
y
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{i}{y}
. From the IH, we have
x,y\notin E_{i}
. So,
x,y\notin E_{i+1}
,
since
E_{i+1}\subseteq E_{i}
by Lemma
46
.

Lemma 50
.

Let
i\leq_{\mathbb{N}}|\textsc{Elim}|
. We have
|E_{i}|=2(|\textsc{Elim}|-i)
.

Proof B.20
.

By induction on
i
.

For the base case
(i=0)
,
E_{0}
consists, by definition, on the domain and codomain of the
Elim
relation. Lemma
45
implies that any two distinct tuples in
Elim
have their
components mutually different. Therefore
|E_{0}|
counts all the events in the first component of tuples in
Elim
plus
all the events in the second component of tuples in
Elim
. In other words,
|E_{0}|
is twice the tuples in
Elim
,
i.e.,
|E_{0}|=2|\textsc{Elim}|
.

For the inductive case,
E_{i+1}
consists, by definition, on
E_{i}
minus the two events in the
i
-th tuple in the enumeration
of
Elim
. In other words,
|E_{i+1}|=|E_{i}|-2
. Therefore, from the IH,

\displaystyle|E_{i+1}|

\displaystyle=|E_{i}|-2

\displaystyle=2(|\textsc{Elim}|-i)-2

\displaystyle=2(|\textsc{Elim}|-i-1)

\displaystyle=2(|\textsc{Elim}|-(i+1))

Lemma 51
.

If
{x}\sqsubset{y}
and
\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {y}})
, then
{\mathcal{S}\ {x}}\sqsubset^{S}{\mathcal{S}\ {y}}
.

Proof B.21
.

Since
{x}\sqsubset{y}
, event
x
has terminated. But
\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {y}})
by hypothesis, so

I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::span-inside-event}}
implies,

{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}.\textit{time}\ \leq_{\mathbb{N}}\ {x}.\textit{end}\ <_{\mathbb{N}}\ {y}.\textit{start}\ \leq_{\mathbb{N}}\ {start\,({\mathcal{S}\ {y}})}.\textit{time}

i.e.,
{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}.\textit{time}\ <_{\mathbb{N}}\ {start\,({\mathcal{S}\ {y}})}.\textit{time}
, which means
end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})<start\,({\mathcal{S}\ {y}})
.
Hence,
\mathcal{S}\ {x}\sqsubset^{S}\mathcal{S}\ {y}
by definition.

B.1.2
Eliding Elimination Pairs

Given the relations in Definition
30
,
in this section we show that all the visibility-style axioms in Figure
3
hold
when all the variables in the axioms range over
\overline{T}\setminus E
, i.e.,
when we elide the elimination pairs.
14
14

14

Axiom
C_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::cc-no-future-dependence}}

has as hypothesis a transitive closure of the constraint relation
x\mathrel{{\prec}^{+}}y
.
We prove that the axiom holds when the transitive closure is computed over the domain

\overline{T}\setminus E
.

Lemma 52
.

The following holds,

1.

Let
x
be a push and
y
a pop. If
(x\notin E\vee y\notin E)
and
\textit{id}\ {x}=\textit{id}\ {y}
, then
\mathcal{S}\ {x}\sqsubset^{S}\mathcal{S}\ {y}
and
at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {y}
.

2.

Let
y
be a pop such that
\textit{defined}\,({at_{s}\ {y}})
. If
y\notin E
, then there is a push
x\notin E
such that

at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {y}
and
\mathcal{S}\ {x}\sqsubset^{S}\mathcal{S}\ {y}
.

Proof B.22
.

We prove each part in turn.

1.

We focus on the
x\notin E
case, since the case
y\notin E
is similar.

By part 1 of Lemma
27
,

{x}\sqsubset{y}
after instantiating with
o\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}y
.
Hypothesis
\textit{id}\ {x}=\textit{id}\ {y}
implies
\textit{defined}\,({\textit{id}\ {y}})
.
So, from
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::pop-ids-imply-span}}

we have
\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {y}})
. Hence, Lemma
51

implies
\mathcal{S}\ {x}\sqsubset^{S}\mathcal{S}\ {y}
.

Finally,
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::timestamps-equal}}
implies

t_{s}\ {x}=t_{s}\ {y}
, which means
at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {y}
.

2.

We know
\textit{defined}\,({at_{s}\ {y}})
, hence
\textit{defined}\,({t_{s}\ {y}})
.
Applying
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::timestamps-imply-span}}
,

I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::span-implies-all-properties}}
, and

I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::pops-take-pushes}}
on
y
, there is a push
u_{y}
such that
\textit{id}\ {u_{y}}=\textit{id}\ {y}
.

Since
y\notin E
, the first part of this lemma implies
at_{s}\ {u_{y}}=at_{s}\ {y}

and
\mathcal{S}\ {u_{y}}\sqsubset^{S}\mathcal{S}\ {y}
.

Also,
u_{y}\notin E
by Lemma
48
,
since
y\notin E
.

Lemma 53
.

Let
x
be any event and
y
a pop. If
\textit{defined}\,({at_{s}\ {x}})
,
\textit{defined}\,({at_{s}\ {y}})
and
x,y\notin E
, then

at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {y}
or
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {x}
or
at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {y}
.

Proof B.23
.

We need to check the case when
x
is a push or a pop.

•

Case
x
is a push.

Since
y\notin E
, Part 2 of Lemma
52

implies that there is a push
u_{y}\notin E
such that
at_{s}\ {u_{y}}=at_{s}\ {y}
.

Therefore, by Pop-Totality, we obtain

at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {u_{y}}
or
at_{s}\ {u_{y}}\ll at_{s}\ {x}

or
at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {u_{y}}
, which means,

at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {y}
or
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {x}
or

at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {y}
, since
at_{s}\ {u_{y}}=at_{s}\ {y}
.

•

Case
x
is a pop.

Since
x,y\notin E
, Part 2 of Lemma
52

implies that there are pushes
u_{x},u_{y}\notin E
such that
at_{s}\ {u_{x}}=at_{s}\ {x}
,
at_{s}\ {u_{y}}=at_{s}\ {y}
.

Therefore, by Pop-Totality, we obtain

at_{s}\ {u_{x}}\ll at_{s}\ {u_{y}}
or
at_{s}\ {u_{y}}\ll at_{s}\ {u_{x}}

or
at_{s}\ {u_{x}}=at_{s}\ {u_{y}}
, which means,

at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {y}
or
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {x}
or

at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {y}
, since
at_{s}\ {u_{x}}=at_{s}\ {x}
and
at_{s}\ {u_{y}}=at_{s}\ {y}
.

Lemma 54
.

Let
x
be a pop. If the following conditions hold,

•

\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
,

•

at_{s}\ {u}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
,

•

at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {u}
,

•

x,u\notin E

then,
{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}
for some pop
o\notin E
such that

at_{s}\ {u}=at_{s}\ {o}
.

Proof B.24
.

After de Morgan manipulations on hypothesis
\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
,
and expanding the definition of
\textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}
, we obtain,
15
15

15

Recall that whenever we negate an atomic formula that contains functions
that could be undefined, like
end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})\not<end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o^{\prime}}})
in our case, we are implicitly negating the
formula
\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})\wedge\textit{defined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o^{\prime}}})\wedge end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})<end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o^{\prime}}})
.

\displaystyle\forall u^{\prime}\notin E.\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}\ \wedge x,u^{\prime}\notin E\wedge at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\implies

\displaystyle\exists o^{\prime}\notin E.\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}=at_{s}\ {o^{\prime}}\wedge(\textit{undefined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o^{\prime}}})\vee\textit{undefined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})\vee{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o^{\prime}}})}\leq{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})})

Instantiating with
u^{\prime}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}u
, and using the second, third and fourth hypotheses (i.e.
at_{s}\ {u}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
,
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {u}
and
x,u\notin E
), we obtain that there is a pop
o\notin E
such that

at_{s}\ {u}=at_{s}\ {o}
and
\textit{undefined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})\vee\textit{undefined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})\vee{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})}\leq{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}
.

The case
\textit{undefined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})\vee\textit{undefined}\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})
is discarded by
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::timestamps-imply-span}}
,
since we know
at_{s}\ {o}
and
at_{s}\ {x}
are defined.

If
end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})=end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})
, then
\mathcal{S}\ {o}=\mathcal{S}\ {x}
by
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::reps-are-injective}}
, which implies
o=x
by
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::spans-are-injective}}
. So,

at_{s}\ {u}=at_{s}\ {o}=at_{s}\ {x}
(since
o=x
). But this contradicts hypothesis

at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {u}
.

Therefore,
{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}
.

Lemma 55
.

Given the domain
\textsc{Ev}\setminus E
for the relation
\prec^{+}
,
if
{x}\prec^{+}{y}
, then one of the following cases hold,

•

x
and
y
are pushes and
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {y}
.

•

x
is a push,
y
is a pop, and,

at_{s}\ {x}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}\vee(at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {x}\ \wedge\ \neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x})

•

x
is a pop,
y
is a push, and
\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
.

•

x
and
y
are pops, and,

\displaystyle(\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y})\ \vee

\displaystyle(at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {x}\ \wedge\ \neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x})

Proof B.25
.

By repeating Lemma
47

on hypothesis
{x}\prec^{+}{y}
, we have

x\mathrel{{\prec_{0}}^{+}}y
in the domain
\textsc{Ev}\setminus E_{0}=\textsc{Ev}\setminus E
.

Denote by
P(x,y)
the statement of the four cases in the lemma we want to prove.

To prove
x\mathrel{{\prec_{0}}^{+}}y\implies P(x,y)
, it
suffices to prove the two properties,

•

(Base case).
x\prec_{0}y\implies P(x,y)
.

•

(Inductive case).
x\prec_{0}y\wedge P(y,z)\implies P(x,z)
.

The base case follows directly from the definitions of
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}^{B}{y}
and
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
,
since they appear in some case of
P(x,y)
.
We now focus on the inductive case. We only need to consider the cases where the types of the events match.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}^{B}{y}
for push
x
and pop
y
, and
\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
for push
z
.

We have
at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {y}
by definition of
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}^{B}{y}
.

But by definition of
\textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}
, we have
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}
. Hence,

at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}

So,
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {z}
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}^{B}{y}
for push
x
and pop
y
, and

\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
for pop
z
.

Similar to the previous case, we will obtain
at_{s}\ {x}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}^{B}{y}
for push
x
and pop
y
, and

at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {y}\ \wedge\ \neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
for pop
z
.

Again by definition of
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}^{B}{y}
, we have
at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {y}
, so,

at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {x}\ \wedge\ \neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
for pushes
x
and
y
, and
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {z}
for push
z
.

From definition of
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
, we have
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {y}
.
Hence,
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {z}
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
for pushes
x
and
y
, and
at_{s}\ {y}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
for pop
z
.

Similarly to the previous case,
at_{s}\ {x}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
for pushes
x
and
y
, and
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {y}\ \wedge\ \neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
for pop
z
.

By Lemma
53
, we have

at_{s}\ {x}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
or
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {x}
.

For the case
at_{s}\ {x}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
we are done.
For the case
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {x}
, we claim
\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
.

For suppose there is such
u^{\prime}
, so
at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
.
But
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {y}
from the hypothesis
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
. Therefore,

at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
, which contradicts
the hypothesis
\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
for pop
x
and push
y
, and
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {z}
for push
z
.

From definition of
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
, we have

\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
. But since
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {z}
,
we have
\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
for pop
x
and push
y
, and
at_{s}\ {y}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
for pop
z
.

By a similar reasoning as in the previous case,
\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
for pop
x
and push
y
, and
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {y}\ \wedge\ \neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
for pop
z
.

From definition of
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
, we have

\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
.
By Lemma
53
,
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {z}
or
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {x}

or
at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {z}
.

–

Case
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {z}
. By
Lemma
53
,
at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}

or
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}
.

*

Case
at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
. Hence, we have
\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
.

*

Case
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}
.

From the hypotheses
at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
and

\neg\exists u^{\prime}.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
and
Lemma
54
, we have

{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o^{\prime}}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {z}})}
for some pop
o^{\prime}\notin E
such that

at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}=at_{s}\ {o^{\prime}}
.

But from hypothesis
\textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}
,
we also have
{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o^{\prime}}})}
. Therefore,
{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {z}})}
.

Since
z\notin E
, Lemma
52
implies that there is a push
u_{z}\notin E

such that
at_{s}\ {u_{z}}=at_{s}\ {z}
.

So,
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {z}=at_{s}\ {u_{z}}
.

We want to show
\textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u_{z}}
, which means it remains
to show
\forall o_{2}\notin E.\ at_{s}\ {u_{z}}=at_{s}\ {o_{2}}\implies{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o_{2}}})}
.
So, let
o_{2}\notin E
such that
at_{s}\ {u_{z}}=at_{s}\ {o_{2}}
. Then,
at_{s}\ {z}=at_{s}\ {u_{z}}=at_{s}\ {o_{2}}
.
Therefore,
z=o_{2}
by
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::prop-funcs-are-injective}}
.
But we already know
{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {z}})}
. So,

{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o_{2}}})}
.

Since
at_{s}\ {u_{z}}=at_{s}\ {z}
, we have,

\exists u_{z}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u_{z}}\wedge at_{s}\ {u_{z}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}

–

Case
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {x}
. We claim that
\neg\exists u_{2}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u_{2}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u_{2}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
. For suppose there is such
u_{2}
. But then from the hypothesis
\textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
,

at_{s}\ {u_{2}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}

contradicting the hypothesis
\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
.

–

Case
at_{s}\ {x}=at_{s}\ {z}
. We get
x=z
from
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::prop-funcs-are-injective}}
, but then
the two hypotheses
\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}

and
\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
are contradictory.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
for pops
x
and
y
, and
\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
for push
z
.

From definition of
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
, we have
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {x}\ \wedge\ \neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
.

Since
x\notin E
, Lemma
52
implies that there is a push
u_{x}\notin E

such that
at_{s}\ {u_{x}}=at_{s}\ {x}
.

From
\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
and

at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {u_{x}}=at_{s}\ {x}
and Lemma
54
,
we obtain
{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o^{\prime}}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {y}})}
for some pop
o^{\prime}\notin E
such that
at_{s}\ {u_{x}}=at_{s}\ {o^{\prime}}
.
Therefore,
o^{\prime}=x
by
I_{\ref{inv::stack::appendix::vis-proof::prop-funcs-are-injective}}
, meaning

{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {y}})}
.

By Lemma
53
,

at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}
or
at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
.

If
at_{s}\ {x}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}
, we have
{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {o_{u^{\prime}}}})}

for any pop
o_{u^{\prime}}
taking
u^{\prime}
, since
{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {x}})}<{end\,({\mathcal{S}\ {y}})}
and
\textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}
.
Therefore
\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {x}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
.

If
at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
, then

\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
which contradicts
hypothesis
\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
for pops
x
and
y
, and
\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {z}
for
pop
z
. This is similar to the previous case.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
for pops
x
and
y
, and
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {y}\ \wedge\ \neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
for
pop
z
.

From definition of
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}^{B}{y}
, we have
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {x}\ \wedge\ \neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {y}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
.

So,
at_{s}\ {z}\ll at_{s}\ {x}
and we claim that

\neg\exists u^{\prime}\notin E.\ \textsc{Miss}\ {z}\ {u^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {x}
. For suppose such
u^{\prime}
exists.

By Lemma
53
,

at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ll$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ll$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}at_{s}\ {y}
or
at_{s}\ {y}\ll at_{s}\ {u^{\prime}}
.

If
