executes before
l
, i.e.,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots(false,0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(true,0)\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u=unlock()\langle tt\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}

then
l
is in between
u_{2}
and
r
, which contradicts
B_{3}
.

If
u_{2}
executes after
l
, i.e.,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(true,0)\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u=unlock()\langle tt\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}

then
u_{2}
is in between
l
and
u
, which contradicts
B_{2}
.

Suppose there is a deregister
d
between
l
and
u
. Axiom
B_{8.4}
states that
d
must be observing
a register
r
that executes before
d
by
B_{7}
. If
r
executes after
l
, i.e.,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(true,0)\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u=unlock()\langle tt\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}

it would contradict
the previous case which states that no register can execute between
l
and
u
. Hence,
r
must execute
before
l
. But
B_{4}
implies that
r
must be deregistered by some
d_{2}
executing before
l
, i.e.,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots(false,0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(true,0)\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u=unlock()\langle tt\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}

and so,
d=d_{2}
by
B_{6}
, which is impossible.

Therefore, the execution must look as in (
20
),
and
u
executes as in
A_{2}^{\prime}
(which is equivalent to
A_{2}
).

Axiom
B_{8.3}
states that either register
r=reg()
executes before any lock in the history
or there is an unlock
u
observed by
r
(which happens when there is some lock executing before
r
).

In case every lock executes after
r
, the execution looks as follows,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,c)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,n+1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r=reg()\langle tt\rangle}

We must have that the boolean does not change from
false
to
true
before
r
, because there is no lock before
r
.
Also, registers and deregisters do not change the boolean. Therefore, just before

r
executes, the boolean is
false
, and
r
executes as in
A_{3}
.

In case
r
observes an unlock
u
, the execution looks as follows,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(true,0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,c)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,n+1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r=reg()\langle tt\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}

First,
u
executes before
r
by
B_{7}
.
u
changes the boolean to
false
.
We must have that the boolean does not change from
false
to
true
in between
u
and
r
,
because
B_{3}
implies that there is no lock in between
u
and
r
.
Also, registers and deregisters do not change the boolean.
Therefore, just before

r
executes, the boolean is
false
, and
r
executes as in
A_{3}
.

Axiom
B_{8.4}
states that any deregister
d=dereg()
must observe a register
r
. The execution
will look as follows,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,c)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,c+1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,n+1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,n)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d=dereg()\langle tt\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}

We claim that before
d
executes, the readers counter must be positive (i.e.,
n+1
, for some
n
).
If the counter is zero, it means that the increase produced by
r
must have been decreased
before

d
executes. But axioms
B_{5}
and
B_{6}
state that increases and decreases are uniquely paired,
meaning that the number of increases must equal the number of decreases before
d
,
because the counter is zero. Hence,
r
must have been deregistered before
d
executed.
But this is impossible, because
d
observes
r
and registers and
deregisters are paired uniquely by
B_{5}
and
B_{6}
.

Also, we claim that there cannot be a lock between
r
and
d
changing the boolean from
false

to
true
. Suppose for a contradiction that there is such lock
l
.

B_{4}
implies that
r
must be deregistered by some
d_{2}
executing before
l
, i.e.,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,c)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,c+1)\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots(false,n+1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(false,n)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d=dereg()\langle tt\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lessdot}

So,
d_{2}=d
by
B_{6}
, which is impossible.

Therefore, just before
d
executes, the boolean is
false
and the readers counter is positive,
so that
d
executes as in
A_{4}^{\prime}
(which is equivalent to
A_{4}
).

(
L_{107}
) No locks in-between

{u_{1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}{l_{1}}\implies\forall l_{2}.\ {l_{2}}\mathrel{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}}{u_{1}}\vee{l_{1}}\mathrel{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}}{l_{2}}

(
L_{108}
) No unlocks in-between

{l_{1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}{u_{1}}\implies\forall u_{2}.\ {u_{2}}\mathrel{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}}{l_{1}}\vee{u_{1}}\mathrel{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}}{u_{2}}

(
L_{109}
) No locks in-between pending reg

{u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{r}\implies\forall l.\ {l}\mathrel{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}}{u}\vee{r}\mathrel{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}}{l}

(
L_{110}
) No pending regs before lock

l\not\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}r\implies\exists d.\ {r}\mathrel{\lessdot}{d}\wedge{d}\mathrel{\ltimes}{l}

(
L_{111}
) Reg uniqueness

{r_{1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}{d}\wedge{r_{2}}\mathrel{\lessdot}{d}\implies r_{1}=r_{2}

(
L_{112}
) Dereg uniqueness

{r}\mathrel{\lessdot}{d_{1}}\wedge{r}\mathrel{\lessdot}{d_{2}}\implies d_{1}=d_{2}

(
L_{113}
) No future dependences

x\mathrel{{\prec}^{+}}y\implies{y}\not\sqsubseteq{x}

(
L_{114}
) Return value completion

\exists v.\ \mathcal{Q}_{{x},{v}}\wedge(x\in T\implies v={x}.\textit{out})

(a)

Concurrent specification. Relations
\lessdot,\ltimes:\textsc{Ev}\times\textsc{Ev}
are abstract.

Constraint relation

{\prec}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\lessdot\cup\ltimes

Returns-before relation

{k_{1}}\sqsubset{k_{2}}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{k_{1}}.\textit{end}<_{\mathbb{N}}{k_{2}}.\textit{start}

Set of terminated events

T\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{k\mid{k}.\textit{end}\neq\bot\}

Closure of terminated events

\overline{T}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{k\mid\exists t\in T.\ k\mathrel{{\prec}^{*}}t\}

\mathcal{Q}_{{l_{1}},{v}}

\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}v=tt\wedge[(\forall l_{2}.\ {l_{1}}\mathrel{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}}{l_{2}})\vee(\exists u.\ {u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{l_{1}})]

\mathcal{Q}_{{u},{v}}

\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}v=tt\wedge\exists l.\ {l}\mathrel{\lessdot}{u}

\mathcal{Q}_{{r},{v}}

\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}v=tt\wedge[(\forall l.\ {r}\mathrel{\ltimes}{l})\vee(\exists u.\ {u}\mathrel{\lessdot}{r})]

\mathcal{Q}_{{d},{v}}

\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}v=tt\wedge\exists r.\ {r}\mathrel{\lessdot}{d}

(b)

Defined notions.

Concurrent Specification

Figure
21
shows the result of
transforming the sequential specification of
Figure
20(c)
into a concurrent
specification. As expected, the transformation generalizes
the returns-before relation by replacing it with an abstract separability relation
\ltimes
.

Axioms
L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::no-lock-in-between}}
-
L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::dereg-uniqueness}}

are the direct result of transforming axioms
B_{1}
-
B_{6}
by applying the
replacement rules described in Section
3.2
:
positive occurrences of the subformula
{a}\sqsubset{b}
are replaced with

{a}\mathrel{\ltimes}{b}
; negative occurrences with
b\not\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}a
.

Axiom
L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::cc-no-future-dependence-locks}}

replaces
B_{7}
. Axiom
L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::cc-no-future-dependence-locks}}

has an identical explanation as axiom
C_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::cc-no-future-dependence}}

in Section
3.2
.

Axiom
L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::cc-return-completion-locks}}

coalesces
B_{8.1}
-
B_{8.4}
into the postcondition predicate
\mathcal{Q}_{{x},{v}}

after replacing the occurrences of
\sqsubset
in
B_{8.1}
-
B_{8.4}
with
\ltimes
.
Axiom
L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::cc-return-completion-locks}}

has an identical explanation as axiom
C_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::cc-return-completion}}

in Section
3.2
.

(A_{1})

false\xrightarrow{lock()\ \langle tt\rangle}true

(A_{2})

true\xrightarrow{unlock()\ \langle tt\rangle}false

Figure 22
:
State-based sequential specification for simple locks. The initial state is
false
.

C.3.2
Simple Locks

A simple lock is a lock that only has the
lock()
and

unlock()
methods. The abstract state of a simple lock is a boolean, where

true
indicates that the lock is acquired, and
false
otherwise.
Figure
22

shows the state-based sequential specification of simple locks.

We get a history-based sequential specification for simple locks
by removing from Figure
20(c)

the axioms
B_{3}
,
B_{4}
,
B_{5}
,
B_{6}
,
B_{8.3}
, and
B_{8.4}
,
all of which talk about registers and deregisters.

Accordingly, we get a concurrent specification for simple locks
simply by removing from Figure
21

the axioms

L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::no-lock-in-between-pending-reg}}
,

L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::no-pending-regs-before-lock}}
,

L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::reg-uniqueness}}
,

L_{\ref{vis-ax::stack::appendix::structures::dereg-uniqueness}}
;
and also, removing the definitions
\mathcal{Q}_{{r},{v}}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{{d},{v}}

for registers and deregisters in the post-condition predicate.
